\delta_{ij}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{r})\equiv\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\delta_{ij}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}.

(414)

The first term is immediately recognized as a
\delta
-function,
so we have

\delta_{ij}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{r})=\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{r})-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}},

(415)

while the second term can be written, for
i\neq j
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}\partial r_{j}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{1}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}\partial r_{j}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dkk^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\phi\int_{0}^{\pi}d\theta\sin\theta\frac{1}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}\partial r_{j}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dk\int_{0}^{\pi}d\theta\sin\theta e^{ikr\cos\theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi{}^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}\partial r_{j}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dk\frac{\sin(kr)}{kr}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi{}^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}\partial r_{j}}\frac{\pi}{2r}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi r^{3}}\frac{3r_{i}r_{j}}{r^{2}},

(416)

and for
i=j
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}^{2}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}^{2}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{1}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi{}^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r_{i}^{2}}\frac{\pi}{2r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\delta(\boldsymbol{r})-\frac{1}{4\pi}\left(\frac{3r_{i}^{2}}{r^{5}}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\delta(\boldsymbol{r})+\frac{1}{4\pi r^{3}}\left(\delta_{ij}-\frac{3r_{i}r_{i}}{r^{2}}\right),

(417)

where we have inserted the second order derivative must be performed
carefully as it needs to be regularized (see
Stewart 2008
;
Frahm 1983
),
and thus we have inserted in the third line
\delta(\boldsymbol{r})/3
.
In the last step we have inserted the Kronecker delta
\delta_{ij}

for convenience, which is possible since we assumed from the start
that
i=j
. We can group the results of equations (
416
)
and (
417
) to recover the result for equation

\delta_{ij}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{r})=\frac{2}{3}\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{r})-\frac{1}{4\pi r^{3}}\left(\delta_{ij}-\frac{3r_{i}r_{j}}{r^{2}}\right).

(418)

The procedure for calculating the longitudinal
\delta
-function
is exactly the same, so we choose to omit it.

G.2
Quantizing the plasmon field

Here we start the quantization procedure of the Plasmon field. If
we promote the vector potential to an operator we have the relation
with the noise operator
\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)
.

\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)=\mu_{0}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime};\omega)\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\delta(z^{\prime}),

(419)

and its transpose

\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)=\mu_{0}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime};\omega)\delta(z^{\prime}).

(420)

In the Weyl gauge, the electric field reads

\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)=i\omega\mu_{0}^{2}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime};\omega)\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\delta(z^{\prime}),

(421)

and its transpose

\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)=i\omega\mu_{0}^{2}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime};\omega)\delta(z^{\prime}).

(422)

The noise operator is assumed to obey commutation relations

\left[\hat{J}_{N,i}(\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega),\hat{J}_{N,j}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})\right]=\mathcal{C}\alpha(\omega)\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}),

(423)

while in the Weyl gauge, since the Lagrangian density of the E-M field
is

\mathcal{L}=(\varepsilon_{0}\dot{\boldsymbol{A}}^{2}+(\nabla\times\boldsymbol{A})^{2}/\mu_{0})/2,

(424)

we can find the canonically conjugate variable to
\boldsymbol{A}

by calculating

\partial\mathcal{L}/\partial\dot{\boldsymbol{A}}=\varepsilon_{0}\dot{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r})=-\varepsilon_{0}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega).

(425)

This means that when we promote the vector potential and the electric
field to operators, we have the canonical commutation relation

\left[\hat{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{E}_{j}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\omega)\right]=i\hbar\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}),

(426)

or since we are interested only in the longitudinal components of
the operators we substitute the full
\delta
-function with

\left[\hat{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{E}_{j}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\omega)\right]=i\hbar\delta_{ij}\delta^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime})

(427)

If we want represent the vector potential in real space and time we
calculate

\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t)=\mu_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega e^{-i\omega t}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)+\mu_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega e^{i\omega t}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega),

(428)

and similarly for the electric field. The commutator of the vector
potential operator and the electric field operator will thus be

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}e^{-i\omega t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\nu}{2\pi}e^{i\nu t}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}_{1}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\left\{\left(i\nu\mu_{0}^{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};\omega)\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1},\omega)\delta(z_{1}),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2},\nu)\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{r}_{2};\nu)\delta(z_{2})\right]+\cdots\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}e^{-i\omega t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\nu}{2\pi}e^{i\nu t}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}_{1}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\left\{i\nu\frac{\mu_{0}}{c^{2}}\delta(z_{1})\delta(z_{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1},\omega),\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2},\nu)\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2};\omega)\right]+\cdots\right\},

(429)

where the Green’s functions are evaluated at
z=0
. The remaining
commutator can be simplified as

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1},\omega),\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2},\nu)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2};\omega)\right]

\displaystyle=\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1},\omega),\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2},\nu)\right]\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2};\omega)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}\alpha(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2})\delta(\omega-\nu)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2};\omega),

(430)

where the constant
\mathcal{C}
and the function
\alpha(\omega)

are to be determined such that the commutator relations of equation
(
423
) are obeyed. Substituting into the
integral, we are left with

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=

\displaystyle\frac{i\mu_{0}\mathcal{C}}{2\pi c^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\omega\alpha(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)

\displaystyle+\cdots.

(431)

The other integral we have so far represented by elipses will contribute
equally to the result, thus we have

\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=2\frac{i\mu_{0}\mathcal{C}}{2\pi c^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\omega\alpha(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega).

(432)

We may now use, as before, only the part of the Green’s function associated
with the SPP field. We thus, focus on performing the integral

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{1}{q}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1})}e^{-qz}}{i\omega\sigma(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})+\omega^{2}/c^{2}}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{p}\frac{1}{p}\frac{e^{-i\boldsymbol{p}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1})}e^{-pz^{\prime}}}{-i\omega\sigma^{*}(\omega)p/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})+\omega^{2}/c^{2}}\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{p})

=\frac{1}{4\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{p}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\frac{1}{qp}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}+i\left(\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{q}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}-i\boldsymbol{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}}e^{-qz}e^{-pz^{\prime}}}{D(q,\omega)D^{*}(p,\omega)}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{p}),

(433)

where
D(q,\omega)
is shorthand for the denominator. Using the fact
that

\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}e^{i\left(\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{q}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}}=(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{q}),

(434)

we have

\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1};\omega)=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{4\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{1}{q^{2}}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}}{\left|i\omega\sigma(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})+\omega^{2}/c^{2}\right|^{2}}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q}).

(435)

The commutator then reads

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=

\displaystyle\frac{i\mu_{0}\pi\mathcal{C}}{c^{2}\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{1}{q^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\omega\alpha(\omega)}{\left|i\omega\sigma(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})+\omega^{2}/c^{2}\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\varepsilon_{0}\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{1}{q^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\omega\alpha(\omega)}{\left|i\omega\sigma(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})+\omega^{2}\right|^{2}}.

(436)

Now we can move on to the evaluation of the integral over the frequency

\mathcal{I}(q)=\int\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\omega\alpha(\omega)}{\left|i\omega\sigma(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})+\omega^{2}\right|^{2}},

(437)

which when considering the complex conductivity
\sigma(\omega)=\sigma_{1}(\omega)+i\sigma_{2}(\omega)

as usual becomes

\mathcal{I}(q)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\omega\alpha(\omega)}{\left|i\omega\sigma_{1}(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})-\omega\sigma_{2}(\omega)q/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}c^{2})+\omega^{2}/c^{2}\right|^{2}}.

(438)

We can yet again evaluate this integral in the complex plane. In particular
we may look at an auxilliary problem where we consider a function

g(z)
such that its contour integral over a large semi-circle in
the upper half-plane vanishes, that is to say

\oint dzg(z)=\lim_{R\to\infty}\left[\int_{-R}^{R}d\omega g(\omega)+\int_{0}^{\pi}d\theta iRe^{i\theta}g(Re^{i\theta})\right]=0,

(439)

which is equivalent to stating that
g(z)
has no poles in the upper
half plane. We therefore know that the integrals are symmetric and
as such, we may evaluate one to find the other. In particular, we
may evaluate pick a
g(z)
of the following form

g(z)=\frac{z}{z^{2}+iqz\sigma(z)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})},

(440)

noting that if

\lim_{R\to\infty}g(Re^{i\theta})=\frac{1}{Re^{i\theta}},

(441)

we have that

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega g(\omega)=-i\pi,

(442)

which folllows directly from equation (
439
), and
noting that if we assume that
iqz\sigma(z)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})

grows more slowly than
z^{2}
(which it does, virtue of the dispersion
relation we had found), then equation (
440
) obeys the
desired limit and thus can be evaluated as in equation (
442
).
Multiplying and dividing by the conjugate of the denominator of
g(\omega),
we
may write

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\omega^{3}-iq\omega^{2}\sigma^{*}(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})}{|\omega^{2}+iq\omega\sigma(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})|^{2}}

\displaystyle=-i\pi

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\omega^{3}-iq\omega^{2}\left[\sigma_{1}(\omega)-i\sigma_{2}(\omega)\right]/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})}{|\omega^{2}+iq\omega\sigma(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})|^{2}}

\displaystyle=-i\pi,

(443)

and if we consider the dispersion relation we found for
\sigma_{2}(\omega)
,
written

\frac{\sigma_{2}(\omega)}{\varepsilon_{0}\omega}=\frac{2\bar{\varepsilon}}{q}

(444)

we may write
\sigma_{2}(\omega)=\frac{2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}\omega}{q}
,
and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\omega^{3}-iq\omega^{2}\left[\sigma_{1}(\omega)-i\sigma_{2}(\omega)\right]/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})}{|\omega^{2}+iq\omega\sigma(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-iq}{2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\sigma_{1}(\omega)\omega^{2}}{|\omega^{2}+iq\omega\sigma(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})|^{2}}

\displaystyle=-i\pi.

(445)

We can now multiply by
-\frac{1}{2\pi i}
on both sides, to obtain

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\sigma_{1}(\omega)\omega^{2}}{|\omega^{2}+iq\omega\sigma(\omega)/(2\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0})|^{2}}=\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}}{q},

and so, we can set
\alpha(\omega)=\omega\sigma_{1}(\omega)
, and
solve the integral of equation (
437
) which (given
that
\sigma_{1}(\omega)
is an even function) will evaluate to

\mathcal{I}(q)=\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}}{2q}.

(446)

Remembering that
\alpha(\omega)
is chosen such that the commutator
becomes a longitudinal
\delta-
function, this choice of
\alpha(\omega)

allows us to write

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\varepsilon_{0}\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}\bar{\varepsilon}\varepsilon_{0}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}}{q^{3}}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q})

\displaystyle=\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}}{q^{3}}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q}).

(447)

Noting that using the general expression for
\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q}),\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q})
,
one can easily verify (in the electrostatic limit) that

\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q})\tilde{G}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{q})=2q^{2}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q}),

(448)

which gives the commutator

\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}}{q}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}\tilde{G}(\boldsymbol{q}).

(449)

We can now consider elements of this commutator, in particular, we
may look at

\left[\hat{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{E}_{j}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{q_{i}q_{j}}{q}e^{i\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})},

(450)

for the longitudinal components of the fields.
i=x,y
. Bringing
the exponentials together, by reintroducing
\boldsymbol{k}\equiv\boldsymbol{p}_{1}=(q_{x},q_{y},i\kappa_{1})\approx(q_{x},q_{y},iq)

and
\boldsymbol{r}=(x,y,z)
and
\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
, we
can write

\displaystyle\left[\hat{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{E}_{j}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{i\pi\mathcal{C}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{q_{i}q_{j}}{q}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)},

(451)

and extending the integral to the whole
q-
space as can easily be
shown by integrating in the complex plane

\displaystyle\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{q^{2}+k_{z}^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)}

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}kk_{i}k_{j}e^{iq(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})}\int dk_{z}\frac{e^{-k_{z}(z+z^{\prime})}}{(q+ik_{z})(q-ik_{z})}

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}kk_{i}k_{j}e^{iq(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})}2\pi i\frac{e^{-q(z+z^{\prime})}}{2qi}

\displaystyle=\pi\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{q}\frac{q_{i}q_{j}}{q}e^{iq(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})}.

(452)

The commutator evaluates to

\left[\hat{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{r},t),-\varepsilon_{0}\hat{E}_{j}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},t)\right]=\frac{i\mathcal{C}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)}.

This is very similar to the for of the longitudinal delta function

\delta_{ij}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime})
. In fact,
recalling that

\delta_{ij}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{r})\equiv\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{k}\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{k^{2}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{r}},

(453)

we can recover equation (
427
) if we let

\mathcal{C}=\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\hbar}{(2\pi)^{3}}.

(454)

Using the form of
\mathcal{C}
and
\alpha(\omega)
we discovered,
the commutators for the noise operators can also be found for
i

and
j=x,y
, we can write

\left[\hat{J}_{N,i}(\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega),\hat{J}_{N,j}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})\right]=\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\hbar}{(2\pi)^{3}}\omega\sigma_{1}(\omega)\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}),

(455)

and therefore we can define the operators

\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)=\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{J}}_{N}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)}{\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\hbar}{(2\pi)^{3}}\omega\sigma_{1}(\omega)}},

(456)

which obey bosonic commutation relations

\left[\hat{\text{f}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{f}}_{i}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right]=\delta_{ij}\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}).

(457)

The vector potential can thus be written making use of these operators
as

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\hbar}{(2\pi)^{3}}\omega\sigma_{1}(\omega)}\int d^{3}\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\delta(z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\equiv\beta(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},z^{\prime}=0;\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega).

(458)

The electric field operator can then be written at the cost of the
vector potential operator in the Schrodinger picture, by Fourier transforming
at
t=0
, giving the quantized field

\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r})=i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)-i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega).

(459)

This result concludes the quantization of the SPP field. In the next
few sections we will aim to use these results, especially the bosonic
creation and annihilation operators of equation (
456
), to
derive the dynamics of two level-systems coupled to graphene plasmons.

Appendix H
Details of the calculation of the dynamics of one qubit coupled to
the plasmonic bath

H.1
Obtaining the dynamics via the Schrodinger equation

In the previous appendices we went over all the building blocks to
set up a mathematical description of our system, in particular the
plasmonic bath. We now move on to a quantitative description of the
interaction between a two-level system and the plasmonic bath. The
Hamiltonian of a two-level system, field and atom-field interaction
is given, in the Schrodinger picture by

\displaystyle\hat{H}=

\displaystyle\hbar\omega_{0}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)

\displaystyle-\sigma_{+}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}_{A},\omega)-\sigma_{-}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{E}}(\boldsymbol{r}_{A},\omega).

(460)

In the rotating wave approximation, we keep only the resonant terms
making up the electric field. The Hamiltonian will therefore be

\displaystyle\hat{H}=

\displaystyle\hbar\omega_{0}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)

\displaystyle-\sigma_{+}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0},\omega)+i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\sigma_{-},

(461)

or at the cost of only the creation and destruction operators

\displaystyle\hat{H}=

\displaystyle\hbar\omega_{0}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)

\displaystyle-\sigma_{+}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},z^{\prime}=0;\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)

\displaystyle+i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta^{*}(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},z^{\prime}=0;\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\sigma_{-}.

(462)

,We therefore have a zeroth order Hamiltonian of the form

\hat{H}_{0}=\hbar\omega_{0}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),

(463)

and an interaction term which can be written as

\displaystyle\hat{V}=

\displaystyle-\sigma_{+}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},z^{\prime}=0;\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)

\displaystyle+i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},z^{\prime}=0;\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\sigma_{-}.

(464)

We can now aim to solve the Schrodinger equation

i\hbar\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle=H\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle.

(465)

To solve the Schrodinger equation we make the following ansatz

\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle=C_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}\left|0\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega e^{-i\omega t}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle.

(466)

Substituting onto the wave equation, on the left we have

\displaystyle i\hbar\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle=i\hbar\dot{C}_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}\left|0\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle+\hbar\omega_{0}C_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}\left|0\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega e^{-i\omega t}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega i\hbar e^{-i\omega t}\dot{\boldsymbol{C}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle.

(467)

We now note that we have written
\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle
as
a function of tensor product states made up of
\left|0\right\rangle,\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle

which describe the plasmonic vacuum and the presence of a single plasmon
in the graphene and the states of the atom
\left|e\right\rangle

and
\left|g\right\rangle
, which are respectively the excited and
ground state for the two-level atom. This way, the operators which
make up the Hamiltonian act in the following ways

\begin{cases}\sigma_{+}\left|i\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle=0,\\
\sigma_{+}\left|i\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle=\left|i\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle,\\
\sigma_{-}\left|i\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle=\left|i\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle,\\
\sigma_{-}\left|i\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle=0,\end{cases}

(468)

\begin{cases}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle=\left|2(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle,\\
\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\left|0\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle=\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle,\\
\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle=\left|0\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle,\\
\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\left|0\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle=0,\end{cases},

(469)

where
i=0,1
and
j=g,e
. Note that since the operators are matched
such that
\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)
is
always paired with
\sigma_{+}
and vice-versa, the state
\left|\boldsymbol{2}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1},\boldsymbol{\rho}_{2},\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right\rangle\left|j\right\rangle

is never actually produced. Acting with the zeroth order Hamiltonian
on the state therefore gives

\hat{H}_{0}\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle=\hbar\omega_{0}C_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}+\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)e^{-i\omega t}\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu,t)\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle,

(470)

where we have also used that
\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu,t)\right\rangle=\left|0\right\rangle\delta(\omega-\nu)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})
.
Using the same properties the interaction Hamiltonian gives

\displaystyle\hat{V}\left|\psi(t)\right\rangle=

\displaystyle-i\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot e^{-i\omega t}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\left|0\right\rangle\left|e\right\rangle

\displaystyle+i\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}C_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}\left|1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)\right\rangle\left|g\right\rangle.

Acting on the left with the bra
\left\langle e\right|\left\langle 0\right|

on the left and hand side of the Schrodinger equation, we have

\displaystyle i\hbar\dot{C}_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}=

\displaystyle-i\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)e^{-i\omega t}\cdot\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t),

or

\dot{C}_{e}(t)=-\frac{1}{\hbar}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})t}\cdot\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t),

(471)

while on the other hand, acting on the left with
\left\langle g\right|\left\langle 1(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right|

we have

\displaystyle\hbar\omega e^{-i\omega t}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)+i\hbar e^{-i\omega t}\dot{\boldsymbol{C}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)=

\displaystyle\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)e^{-i\omega t}

\displaystyle+i\omega\beta(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{0}C_{e}(t)e^{-i\omega_{0}t}.

(472)

\Leftrightarrow\dot{\boldsymbol{C}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)=\frac{1}{\hbar}\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)t}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t).

(473)

Integrating the previous equations with respect to time, choosing

C_{e}(0)=1
and
\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,0)=\boldsymbol{0}

we have

C_{e}(t)=1-\frac{1}{\hbar}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})t_{1}}\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t_{1}),

(474)

\boldsymbol{C}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega,t)=\frac{1}{\hbar}\omega\beta(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)t_{1}}C_{e}(t_{1}).

(475)

We can in particular, substitute equation (
475
) into equation
(
471
), to obtain the differential equation

\displaystyle\dot{C}_{e}(t)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})t}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{\hbar}\omega\beta(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)t_{1}}C_{e}(t_{1}).

(476)

We can simplify the previous expression as

\displaystyle\dot{C}_{e}(t)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega^{2}\beta^{2}(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)

\displaystyle\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})t}\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)t_{1}}C_{e}(t_{1}),

(477)

and we can further introduce the kernel (see appendix
F.11
)

\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)
defined by

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}\mu_{0}\omega^{2}\frac{\sigma_{1}(\omega)}{\varepsilon_{0}\omega}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{2}}{c^{2}}\frac{\sigma_{1}(\omega)}{\varepsilon_{0}\omega}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)

\displaystyle=\text{Im}[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega)],

(478)

We shall discuss the evaluation of this integral in due time, but
first we move on to write

\dot{C}_{e}(t)=-\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)(t_{1}-t)}C_{e}(t_{1}).

(479)

We can now make the first Markov approximation to write
C_{e}(t_{1})\approx C_{e}(t)
.
We then have

\displaystyle\dot{C}_{e}(t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}C_{e}(t)\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega_{0}-\omega)(t_{1}-t)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\omega^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}C_{e}(t).

(480)

By the Sokhotski–Plemelj identity the integral is

\mathcal{I}_{SP}(\omega)=\pi\delta(\omega-\omega_{0})+i\mathcal{P}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega_{0}}

(481)

Using this result, we have

\dot{C_{e}}(t)=iC_{e}(t)\left[-\Delta+i\Gamma\right],

(482)

where we have introduced the Lamb Shift

\Delta=\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\mathcal{P}\int d\omega\omega^{2}\frac{\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\text{Im}[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega)]\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}}{\omega-\omega_{0}},

(483)

and the decay rate

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle=-\pi\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\int d\omega\omega^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\text{Im}[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega)]\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\delta(\omega-\omega_{0})

\displaystyle=-\pi\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar\varepsilon_{0}c^{2}}\omega_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\text{Im}[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega_{0})]\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}.

(484)

We will now compute this rate using the dispersion relation

H.1.1
Computation of the decay rate of a single qubit

We now aim to calculate the decay rate for our two-level system close
to the Graphene sheet. We therefore will make use of the Green’s function
we found previously in equation (
35
),
to calculate

\text{Im}[\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega_{0})]=c^{2}\frac{\pi^{2}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}\frac{q_{\text{spp}}^{2}}{A(\omega_{0})}\text{Im}[\bar{M}(q_{\text{ssp}},0,0)],

(485)

where
A
is in general, a function of
\omega_{0}
. The imaginary
part of the evaluated matrix gives

\begin{cases}\text{Im}\left[M_{11}\right]=-J_{0}\left(q_{\text{spp}}R\right)+\frac{J_{1}\left(q_{\text{spp}}R\right)}{q_{\text{spp}}R},\\
\text{Im}\left[M_{22}\right]=-\frac{J_{1}\left(q_{\text{spp}}R\right)}{q_{\text{spp}}R},\\
\text{Im}\left[M_{33}\right]=-J_{0}\left(q_{\text{spp}}R\right),\end{cases}

(486)

and

\begin{cases}\text{Im}\left[M_{12}\right]=\text{Im}\left[M_{21}\right]=0,\\
\text{Im}\left[M_{13}\right]=\text{Im}\left[M_{31}\right]=J_{1}\left(q_{\text{spp}}R\right),\\
\text{Im}\left[M_{23}\right]=\text{Im}\left[M_{32}\right]=0.\end{cases}.

(487)

Therefore, we have, if we let
a\equiv q_{\text{spp}}R
for the sake
of simplifying the notation, we find

\Gamma=-\pi\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar\varepsilon_{0}c^{2}}\omega_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\text{Im}[\bar{G}_{1}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r};\omega_{0})]\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi\bar{\varepsilon}\mu_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}\hbar}\omega_{0}^{2}\left(-c^{2}\frac{\pi^{2}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}\frac{q_{\text{spp}}^{2}}{A(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\left[\lim_{a\to 0}\begin{pmatrix}J_{0}\left(a\right)-\frac{J_{1}\left(a\right)}{a}&0&-J_{1}\left(a\right)\\
0&\frac{J_{1}\left(a\right)}{a}&0\\
-J_{1}\left(a\right)&0&J_{0}\left(a\right)\end{pmatrix}\right]\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}.

(488)

Introducing the notation

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle=c^{2}\frac{\mu_{0}}{8\hbar}\omega_{0}^{2}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}\frac{q_{\text{spp}}^{2}}{A(\omega_{0})}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1/2&0&0\\
0&1/2&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\hbar\varepsilon_{0}}\frac{\omega_{0}^{2}}{A(\omega_{0})}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}q_{\text{spp}}^{2}\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&2\end{pmatrix}\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\hbar\varepsilon_{0}}q_{\text{spp}}^{3}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}(d_{eg}^{\parallel 2}+2d_{eg}^{\perp 2}),

(489)

and writing

q_{\text{spp}}^{3}=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{\lambda_{\text{spp}}^{3}},

(490)

we have a result compatible with the literature

\Gamma=\frac{1}{16\hbar\varepsilon_{0}}\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{\lambda_{\text{spp}}^{3}}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}(d_{eg}^{\parallel 2}+2d_{eg}^{\perp 2})

(491)

or by writing
\Gamma=\gamma/2
, we find the rate

\gamma=\frac{1}{8\hbar\varepsilon_{0}}\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{\lambda_{\text{spp}}^{3}}e^{-2q_{\text{spp}}z}(d_{eg}^{\parallel 2}+2d_{eg}^{\perp 2}).

(492)

This is the final result for the rate
\gamma
that characterizes
the spontaneous decay of a two-level system coupled to an SPP field.

H.2
Deriving the Lindblad equation and computing the dynamics of a single
qubit

We now aim to present a microscopic derivation of the Lindblad equation
for our system. This will allow us to extend these calculations to
the case where both qubits are excited, but we first present a derivation
for a single qubit coupled to the SPP field. As with the case of the
thermal EM field, we start with noting that for interaction Hamiltonians
of the form

\hat{V}=\sum_{i}\beta_{i}\otimes\Gamma_{i},

(493)

it is possible to write the Redfield equation in terms of the reservoir
correlation functions as

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\sum_{ij}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}d\tau\left(\beta_{i}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)-\beta_{j}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{i}(t)\right)\left\langle\Gamma_{i}(t)\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\beta_{i}(t)-\beta_{i}(t)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\right)\left\langle\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\Gamma_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{R}.

(494)

These reservoir correlation functions are defined as

\begin{cases}\left\langle\Gamma_{i}(t)\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\right\rangle\equiv\text{tr}_{R}\left\{\hat{\rho}_{R}\Gamma_{i}(t)\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\right\},\\
\left\langle\Gamma_{j}(t)\Gamma_{i}(\tau)\right\rangle\equiv\text{tr}_{R}\left\{\hat{\rho}_{R}\Gamma_{j}(t)\Gamma_{i}(\tau)\right\}.\end{cases}

(495)

The Hamiltonian for a single qubit are given by equation (
603
)
if the indices
\alpha
are all set to
1
. Since the commutation
relations for SPP creation and destruction operators follow directly
from equation (
469
), we can find the operators in the
interaction picture via

\displaystyle\hat{\text{{f}}}_{I}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)

\displaystyle=e^{i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar}.

We make use of the commutator property
Ae^{B}=e^{c}e^{B}A
, where

\left[A,B\right]=cA
. In this case, we have

e^{i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar}=e^{c}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),

(496)

where

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),-it/\hbar\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\nu\hbar\nu\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]

\displaystyle=-it\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\nu\nu\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right].

(497)

The commutator can be expanded as

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)+\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]

\displaystyle=-\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\nu),

(498)

from which we have

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar\right]

\displaystyle=it\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\nu\nu\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\nu)

\displaystyle=i\omega t\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega).

(499)

Thus, we can use the privously mentioned identity, with
c=i\omega t
.
The result is the expected

\hat{\text{{f}}}_{I}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)=\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{i\omega t}.

(500)

A similar calculation yields

\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar\right]=\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\nu\nu\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right],

(501)

where the commutator yields

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)+\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\right]

\displaystyle=\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\nu)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\nu),

(502)

from where follows

\left[\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),-i\hat{H}_{0}t/\hbar\right]=-i\omega t\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),

(503)

which gives the representation

\hat{\text{{f}}}_{I}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)=\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{-i\omega t}.

(504)

As we have seen before, we also have for the qubit raising and lowering
operators

\begin{cases}\sigma_{+}^{I}=\sigma_{+}e^{i\omega_{0}t},\\
\sigma_{-}^{I}=\sigma_{-}e^{-i\omega_{0}t},\end{cases}

(505)

We can now identify the Hamiltonian of SPP field coupled to the qubit
as one of the form (
493
), by making the definitions

\begin{cases}\hbar\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\equiv\omega\beta(\omega)\boldsymbol{d}\cdot\bar{\bar{G}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega),\\
\hbar\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\equiv\omega\beta^{*}(\omega)\bar{\bar{G}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho},z=0;\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}^{\dagger},\end{cases}

and then

\begin{cases}\beta_{1}=\sigma_{-},\\
\beta_{2}=\sigma_{+},\\
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma^{\dagger}=i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega),\\
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma=-i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega),\end{cases}

(506)

with which the interaction Hamiltonian takes a form identical to that
we had previously obtained when treating the thermal E-M field

\hat{V}=\hbar\left(\sigma_{-}\Gamma^{\dagger}+\sigma_{+}\Gamma\right).

The procedure to derive the Lindblad equation is therefore very similar
to the case of the thermal E-M field. We can write all the operators
in the interaction picture

\begin{cases}\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)=\sigma_{-}e^{-i\omega_{0}t},\\
\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)=\sigma_{+}e^{i\omega_{0}t},\\
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)=i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)e^{i\omega t},\\
\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)=-i\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{-i\omega t}.\end{cases}

(507)

The summation present in the Redfield equation is over
i=1,2
and

j=1,2
and therefore, we can evaluate all the reservoir correlation
functions in the interaction picture

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\text{tr}_{R}\left\{\hat{\rho}_{R}\tilde{\Gamma}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}(\tau)\right\}

\displaystyle=\text{tr}_{R}\left\{-\hat{\rho}_{R}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)e^{i\omega t}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}\right\}

\displaystyle=0,

(508)

in the rotating wave approximation. Similarly

\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle=0.

(509)

The story becomes much more complicated, however, for the remaining
reservoir correlation functions
\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle

and
\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle
.
We will therefore consider first the case of zero temperature, which
is much simpler and will allow us to recover the results obtained
in the Schrodinger picture. Then, we will move on to a description
at finite temperature.

H.3
Evaluating the correlation functions at temperature
T=0
: Density
matrix
\rho_{R}=|0\rangle\langle 0|

In this appendix we provide the aforementioned zero temperature description
of the The reason for choosing the temperature
T=0
is that the
density matrix of the reservoir will be given by

\rho_{R}=|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|=|0\rangle\langle 0|,

(510)

since it has not been perturbed by the decay of the qubit. We can
also work in the subspace of the reservoir with basis
\left\{\left|0\right\rangle,\left|\mathds{1}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right\rangle\right\}
,
since at most there will be a single SPP present. In this subspace,
the trace will in principle be calculated over two terms, however
we see that one of these terms immediately vanishes due to the form
of the density matrix. This results in

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\text{tr}_{R}\left\{\hat{\rho}_{R}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\}

\displaystyle=\braket{0|0}\left\langle 0\right|\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)e^{i\omega t}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}\left|0\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0.

The other correlation function is given by

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\text{tr}_{R}\left\{\hat{\rho}_{R}\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\}

\displaystyle=\braket{0|0}\left\langle 0\right|\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)e^{i\omega t}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}\left|0\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\left\langle\mathds{1}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\right|e^{-i\omega t}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\left|\mathds{1}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}e^{-i\omega t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)e^{i\omega(\tau-t)}.

(511)

Similarly

\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\right\rangle=\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle^{*}.

Substituting into the Redfield equation (
539
),
all terms but those proportional to this correlation function vanish,
and therefore we are left with

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}d\tau\left(\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{R},

(512)

and substituting the operators, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}e^{i\omega_{0}(t-\tau)}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}e^{i\omega_{0}(t-\tau)}\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}e^{i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}e^{i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle^{*}.

(513)

If we introduce the notation

\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\equiv\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega).

(514)

We can perform the Markov approximation, extending the integral in
time to infinity and writing
\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t_{1})\approx\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)
.
This yields

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt_{1}e^{i(\omega-\omega_{0})(t_{1}-t)}

\displaystyle-\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt_{1}e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})(t_{1}-t)},

(515)

and we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\mathcal{I}(\omega)

\displaystyle-\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\mathcal{I}^{*}(\omega),

(516)

where

\mathcal{I}(\omega)=\pi\delta(\omega-\omega_{\alpha^{\prime}})-i\mathcal{P}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega_{\alpha^{\prime}}}.

(517)

We can therefore define

\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\mathcal{I}(\omega)\equiv\Gamma+i\Delta,

(518)

which lets us write

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)(\Gamma+i\Delta)

\displaystyle-\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)(\Gamma-i\Delta).

(519)

We can write this more compactly as

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}+\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\Gamma

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)i\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Gamma\left(\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}-2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)-i\Delta\left(\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]\right).

We now want to return to the Shcrodinger picture, and therefore use
the definition of the operator in the interaction picture

\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)=e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}\hat{\rho}_{S}e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar},

(520)

with which we can calculate the derivative

\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar}+\frac{i}{\hbar}e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}\left[H_{0},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right]e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar},

(521)

and can further rewrite

\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar}\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[H_{0},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

(522)

We can now convert from the interaction picture to the Schrodinger
picture, by writing

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\Gamma\left(e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar}\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}-e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar}2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}\right)

\displaystyle-i\Delta\left(e^{-iH_{0}t/\hbar}\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]e^{iH_{0}t/\hbar}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[H_{0},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\Gamma\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)-i\Delta\left(\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right]\right)-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[H_{0},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

(523)

We can now define

\begin{cases}\hat{L}=\sqrt{\Gamma}\sigma_{-},\\
\hat{L}^{\dagger}=\sqrt{\Gamma}\sigma_{+},\end{cases}

(524)

which yields

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}

\displaystyle=2\hat{L}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\hat{L}^{\dagger}-\left\{\hat{L}^{\dagger}\hat{L},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[(\omega+\Delta)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

(525)

This is an equation in Lindblad form, and we now check that the dynamics
reproduces those found via Schrodinger’s equation. We write the previous
equation in the convenient form

\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=\Gamma\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[\hbar(\omega_{0}+\Delta)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

We now expand the density matrix as

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{S}

\displaystyle=\sum_{ij}\rho_{ij}(t)\left|i\right\rangle\left\langle j\right|,

(526)

but look only at the populations
P_{i}=\rho_{ii}
. To do so, we
substitute this onto the differential equation above and act on the
left and right with the bra and ket corresponding to the state
\left|e\right\rangle
.
This yields

\displaystyle\dot{P}_{e}(t)=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\left\langle e\right|2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\left|e\right\rangle-\left\langle e\right|\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\left|e\right\rangle-\left\langle e\right|\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\left|e\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\hbar}(\omega_{0}+\Delta)\left(\left\langle e\right|\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\left|e\right\rangle-\left\langle e\right|\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\left|e\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-P_{e}-P_{e}\right)-\frac{i}{\hbar}(\omega_{0}+\Delta)(P_{e}-P_{e})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Gamma P_{e}(t).

(527)

While for the ground state

\displaystyle\dot{P}_{g}(t)=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\left\langle g\right|2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\left|g\right\rangle-\left\langle g\right|\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\left|g\right\rangle-\left\langle g\right|\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\left|g\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\hbar}(\omega_{0}+\Delta)\left(\left\langle g\right|\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\left|g\right\rangle-\left\langle g\right|\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\left|g\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Gamma P_{e}(t).

(528)

This yields the same dynamics as we had obtained via Schrodinger’s
equation, namely

P_{e}(t)=P_{e}(0)e^{-2\Gamma t}.

(529)

and for the ground state

\displaystyle P_{g}(t)

\displaystyle=2\Gamma\int_{0}^{t}P_{e}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2\Gamma P_{e}(0)\frac{e^{-2\Gamma t}-1}{-2\Gamma}

\displaystyle=P_{e}(0)\left(1-e^{-2\Gamma t}\right)

(530)

Setting
\gamma=2\Gamma
we obtain

P_{g}(t)=P_{e}(0)\left(1-e^{-\gamma t}\right).

(531)

Note that
\gamma
has exactly of the same form as derived in appendix

H.1.1
.

H.4
Evaluating the correlation functions at a finite temperature
T

The Lindblad approach has the advantage of being able to derive these
results for a finite temperature aswell, where the SPP field will
be described by a bosonic distribution

\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)=\frac{1}{e^{\hbar\omega/k_{B}T}-1}.

(532)

We will use the following results found in
Scheel and Buhmann 2009
.

\begin{cases}\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}=0,\\
\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}=0,\\
\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}=\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}),\\
\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}=\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)+1\right]\delta(\boldsymbol{\rho}-\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime})\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}).\end{cases}.

(533)

The derivation of the Lindblad equation proceeds exactly as described
in section
B.2
, and as before we
have only to derive the reservoir correlation functions

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)e^{-i\omega^{\prime}\tau}e^{i\omega t}\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle=0.

(534)

Similarly, using
\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle=0
,
we find

\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle=0.

(535)

Therefore, we have to calculate only

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})e^{-i\omega t}e^{i\omega^{\prime}\tau}\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)+1\right]e^{i\omega(\tau-t)}.

(536)

In the same manner, we can find

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega^{\prime}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime};\omega^{\prime})e^{i\omega t}e^{-i\omega^{\prime}\tau}\left\langle\hat{\text{{f}}}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{\rho},\omega)\hat{\text{{f}}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)e^{-i\omega(\tau-t)}.

(537)

We also have

\begin{cases}\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{R}=\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}^{*},\\
\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\right\rangle_{R}=\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}^{*}.\end{cases}

(538)

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\sum_{ij}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}d\tau\left(\beta_{i}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)-\beta_{j}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{i}(t)\right)\left\langle\Gamma_{i}(t)\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\beta_{i}(t)-\beta_{i}(t)\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\beta_{j}(\tau)\right)\left\langle\Gamma_{j}(\tau)\Gamma_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{R}.

(539)

Therefore, the Redfield equation reads,

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\left(\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{2}(\tau)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(t)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)-\tilde{\beta}_{2}(t)\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\tilde{\beta}_{1}(\tau)\right)\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\right\rangle_{R},

(540)

and using the previous definitions for the operators
\beta_{i}

and
\Gamma_{i}
we write it as

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)e^{-i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)e^{i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}^{*}

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\right)e^{i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\right)e^{-i\omega_{0}(\tau-t)}\left\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}(t)\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}(\tau)\right\rangle_{R}^{*}.

(541)

Substituting the previously evaluated reservoir correlation functions,
and using the notation

\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\equiv\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\int d^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}\mathcal{G}^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega)\mathcal{G}^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{\rho};\omega),

(542)

we write

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\left(\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)+1\right]\int_{0}^{t}d\tau e^{i(\omega-\omega_{0})(\tau-t)}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\right)\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)+1\right]\int_{0}^{t}d\tau e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})(\tau-t)}

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\right)\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{0})(\tau-t)}

\displaystyle+\left(\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\right)\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau e^{i(\omega-\omega_{0})(\tau-t)}.

(543)

Letting the integral go to infinity and using the Sokhotski-Plemelj,
we can define

\begin{cases}\Gamma\equiv\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\delta(\omega-\omega_{0}),\\
\Delta\equiv\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}}{\omega_{0}-\omega},\\
\Delta^{\prime}\equiv\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\frac{\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\boldsymbol{r},\omega)\cdot\boldsymbol{d}_{eg}\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega)}{\omega_{0}-\omega}.\end{cases}

(544)

to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right)\left[\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]+i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\right)\left[\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]-i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\text{+}\left(\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right)\left[\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})-i\Delta^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\right)\left[\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+i\Delta^{\prime}\right].

(545)

Grouping together all the terms in , we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)+\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\right)

\displaystyle+i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\left(\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}+\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)+\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\right)

\displaystyle-i\Delta^{\prime}\left(\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)-\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}+\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}\right),

(546)

which can be compacted as

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+i\Delta^{\prime}\left[\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right].

(547)

We now note that the
\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}
can be written as

\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+}=\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\left[\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}\right],

(548)

where this commutator can be calculated employing

\begin{cases}\sigma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(\sigma_{x}-i\sigma_{y}),\\
\sigma_{+}=\frac{1}{2}(\sigma_{x}+i\sigma_{y}),\end{cases}

(549)

and the commutator relations of the Pauli matrices

\left[\sigma_{i},\sigma_{j}\right]=2i\varepsilon_{ijk}\sigma_{k}.

(550)

This yields

\displaystyle\left[\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left[\sigma_{x}-i\sigma_{y},\sigma_{x}+i\sigma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left[\sigma_{x},\sigma_{x}\right]+i\left[\sigma_{x},\sigma_{y}\right]-i\left[\sigma_{y},\sigma_{x}\right]+\left[\sigma_{y},\sigma_{y}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}\left[\sigma_{x},\sigma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{z}.

(551)

Substituting this into our dynamical equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+i\Delta^{\prime}\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\sigma_{z},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right].

(552)

We can also use the fact that

\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathds{1}+\sigma_{z}\right),

(553)

or rather, that

\sigma_{z}=2\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-\mathds{1}.

(554)

This yields

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+i\Delta^{\prime}\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}-2\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}+\mathds{1},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right],

(555)

and since
\left[\mathds{1},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-i\Delta^{\prime}\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right],

(556)

which finally yields

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+2\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}^{I}(t)\right].

(557)

We can convert this into the Schrodinger picture, and the procedure
is similar to that carried out in detail for a single qubit. In the
end, we simply get rid of the
I
index and add a term corresponding
to the coherent evolution of the system. This procedure results in

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\Delta+2\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right]-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[H_{0},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

(558)

Since
H_{0}=\hbar\omega_{0}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-}
, we can write compactly

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\left(2\sigma_{-}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{+}-\left\{\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(2\sigma_{+}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\sigma_{-}-\left\{\sigma_{-}\sigma_{+},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\omega_{0}+\Delta+2\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right].

(559)

Defining the operators

\begin{cases}\hat{L}_{1}=\sqrt{\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})}\sigma_{\text{+}},\\
\hat{L}_{2}=\sqrt{\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]}\sigma_{-},\end{cases}

(560)

we can write the equation in Lindblad form

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\left(2\hat{L}_{2}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\hat{L}_{2}^{\dagger}-\left\{\hat{L}_{2}^{\dagger}\hat{L}_{2},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(2\hat{L}_{1}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\hat{L}_{1}^{\dagger}-\left\{\hat{L}_{1}^{\dagger}\hat{L}_{1},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-i(\omega_{0}+\Delta+2\Delta^{\prime})\left[\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right],

(561)

or introducing a sum over
k=1,2
, the result

\frac{d\hat{\rho}_{S}}{dt}=-\frac{i}{\hbar}\left[\hbar\omega_{0}^{\prime}\sigma_{+}\sigma_{-},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right]+\sum_{k=1,2}\left(2\hat{L}_{k}\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\hat{L}_{k}^{\dagger}-\left\{\hat{L}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{L}_{k},\hat{\rho}_{S}(t)\right\}\right),

(562)

with
\omega_{0}^{\prime}=\omega_{0}+\Delta+\Delta^{\prime}
. We can convert this
into a dynamical equation for the probability of finding each state
by proceeding analogously to the case of a single qubit. We write

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{S}

\displaystyle=\rho_{ee}\left|e\right\rangle\left\langle e\right|+\rho_{eg}\left|e\right\rangle\left\langle g\right|+\rho_{ge}\left|g\right\rangle\left\langle e\right|+\rho_{gg}\left|g\right\rangle\left\langle g\right|

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\rho_{ee}&\rho_{eg}\\
\rho_{ge}&\rho_{gg}\end{bmatrix}.

(563)

We can write the Lindblad jump operators in matrix form

\begin{cases}\hat{L}_{1}&=\sqrt{\Gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})}\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},\\
\hat{L}_{2}&=\sqrt{\Gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]}\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.\end{cases}

(564)

We can now substitute these objects into the Lindblad equation and
setting
\gamma=2\Gamma
, we find

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\rho}_{ee}&\dot{\rho}_{eg}\\
\dot{\rho}_{ge}&\dot{\rho}_{gg}\end{bmatrix}=-i4\omega_{0}\begin{bmatrix}0&\rho_{eg}\\
\rho_{ge}&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}-\gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\rho_{ee}+\gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\rho_{gg}&-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left(\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]+\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\right)\rho_{eg}\\
-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left(\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]+\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\right)\rho_{ge}&\gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]\rho_{ee}-\gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\rho_{gg}\end{bmatrix}.

(565)

Substituting into equation (
559
) and looking at the
diagonal elements which correspond to the populations, we obtain a
coupled set of differential equations for the probabilities

\begin{cases}\dot{P}_{e}=-\gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]P_{e}+\gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})P_{g},\\
\dot{P}_{g}=\gamma\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]P_{e}-\gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})P_{g}.\end{cases}

(566)

This is slightly more complicated then the case at zero temperature
since the qubits may also be excited by thermal fluctuations of the
SPP field. These differential equations have a general solution given
by

\displaystyle P_{e}(t)=

\displaystyle C_{1}\frac{\gamma\left(\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+\left[\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]e^{-\gamma(2\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1)t}\right)}{\gamma\left[2\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]}

\displaystyle+C_{2}\frac{\gamma\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})\left(-1+e^{-\gamma(2\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1)t}\right)}{\gamma\left[2\bar{n}_{\text{spp}}(\omega_{0})+1\right]},

(567)

\displaystyle P_{g}(t)=
